## 4Hardness whenλ>λc\lambda>\lambda_{c}

We prove the hardness part of Theorem1.2by adapting the phase-coexistence gadget framework introduced by Sly[36]for the hard-core model above the tree uniqueness threshold and subsequently used in several hardness reductions for two-spin systems[37,18,17,6]. The basic building block is a random bipartiteΔ\Delta-regulargadget, modified by deleting a small number of matching edges and attaching finite(Δ−1)(\Delta-1)-ary trees in order to create terminal vertices. In the non-uniqueness regimeλ>λc​(Δ)\lambda>\lambda_{c}(\Delta), the hard-core model on such a gadget has two dominant phases: in the (+)-phase the left side of the core is more heavily occupied, while in the (−-)-phase the right side is more heavily occupied. Conditioned on either phase, the terminal occupations are approximately independent with phase-dependent marginals.

In our proof, the role of the global balance constraint is similar to that of the fixed-magnetization in the Ising model in[6].
Given an input graphHH, we replace each vertexx∈V​(H)x\in V(H)by a copyGxG_{x}of the gadget.
A phase vectorY∈{+,−}V​(H)Y\in\{+,-\}^{V(H)}therefore assigns a phase to each gadget copy, and hence plays the role of a spin configuration onHH.
We then join terminal vertices of different gadget copies according to the edges ofHH, and also add isolated vertices to provide an independent source of fluctuation for the global balance variable. The first part of the proof analyzes the disjoint unionH^sG\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}, before the inter-gadget edges are added.
In this decoupled graph, the gadget copies are independent, and we prove
concentration and point probability estimates for the global imbalanceB​(I)=|I∩L​(H^sG)|−|I∩R​(H^sG)|.B(I)=|I\cap L(\widehat{H}_{s}^{G})|-|I\cap R(\widehat{H}_{s}^{G})|.These estimates show that, after conditioning on exact balanceB​(I)=0B(I)=0, phase vectors with unequal numbers of(+)(+)and(−)(-)gadgets are exponentially suppressed, while phase vectors with equal numbers of(+)(+)and(−)(-)gadgets contribute at scale at leaste−O​(h​log⁡n)​(n​h)−1/2e^{-O(h\log n)}(nh)^{-1/2}. This weaker lower bound is still sufficient because it creates only anO​(h​log⁡n)O(h\log n)error after taking logarithms.

The second part of the proof reintroduces the inter-gadget edges. Since the terminal spins are approximately independent under the phase-conditioned measures, the effect of the inter-gadget edges can be computed explicitly in terms of the phase vectorYY. With our choice of terminal matchings, an edge ofHHwhose endpoints have equal phases receives a larger compatibility weight than an edge whose endpoints have opposite phases. Consequently, among the balanced phase vectors, the dominant contribution to the balanced partition function comes from those minimizing the number of cut edges inHH. Thus the balanced hard-core partition function onHsGH_{s}^{G}encodes the minimum bisection value ofHH. ComparingZHsGbalZ^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H_{s}^{G}}with the partition functionZH^sGZ_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}then allows us to recover the minimum bisection value from sufficiently accurate multiplicative approximations, giving the desired hardness reduction.

The remaining task is to estimate the partition function of the decoupled graph. We do this without using existing algorithms for random bipartite graphs, whose available guarantees in the present regime requireΔ\Deltasufficiently large. Here, instead, the expected partition function of a single gadget can be computed explicitly, and small subgraph conditioning shows that a typical gadget differs from this expectation by at most a polynomial factor. This loss is small enough for the reduction.

We will use the following standard hardness fact.

## Fact 4.1([20]).

The exactMIN-BISECTIONproblem isNP-hard: given a graphHHon an even number of vertices, it isNP-hardto compute the minimum number of edges crossing a bisection ofV​(H)V(H).

The main technical result of this section is the following reduction. Theorem1.2, in the non-uniqueness regime, follows immediately from Theorem4.2and Fact4.1.

## Theorem 4.2.

FixΔ≥3\Delta\geq 3andλ>λc​(Δ)\lambda>\lambda_{c}(\Delta). There existsζ>0\zeta>0such that the following holds. If there is a randomized polynomial-time algorithm which, on everyNN-vertex input graphF∈𝒢Δ,ΔF\in\mathcal{G}_{\Delta,\Delta}, gives aneNζe^{N^{\zeta}}-factor approximation toZFbal​(λ)Z^{\text{bal}}_{F}(\lambda), meaning that its outputZ^\widehat{Z}satisfiese−Nζ​ZFbal​(λ)≤Z^≤eNζ​ZFbal​(λ)e^{-N^{\zeta}}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{F}(\lambda)\leq\widehat{Z}\leq e^{N^{\zeta}}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{F}(\lambda)

with probability at least2/32/3, thenMIN-BISECTIONcan be solved in randomized polynomial time. Moreover, if there is an efficient sampling scheme forμF,λbal\mu^{\text{bal}}_{F,\lambda}on allF∈𝒢Δ,ΔF\in\mathcal{G}_{\Delta,\Delta}, thenMIN-BISECTIONcan be solved in randomized polynomial time.

## Proof.

Recall thatBgad=XL−XRB_{\mathrm{gad}}=X_{L}-X_{R}whereXL:=|I∩UL|X_{L}:=|I\cap U^{L}|andXR:=|I∩UR|X_{R}:=|I\cap U^{R}|.
By Lemma4.4, with probability at least19/2019/20over the choice ofGG, (4.3)-(4.7) hold forXLX_{L}andας​n\alpha_{\varsigma}n.

To pass from the corresponding bounds forXL,ας​nX_{L},\alpha_{\varsigma}nto those forXR,ας¯​nX_{R},\alpha_{\bar{\varsigma}}n, we use the left–right symmetry of the gadget. Indeed, interchanging the two bipartition classes sends the random gadget to one with the same distribution, while exchanging the roles of the two phases (including flipping the auxiliary sign on a tie) and replacing the left-core count by the right-core count. Accordingly, the statement of the previous lemma for the left side immediately yields the analogous statement for the right side, with the centerας​n\alpha_{\varsigma}nreplaced byας¯​n\alpha_{\bar{\varsigma}}n. We may therefore assume that the same moment and exponential moment bounds hold simultaneously for bothXLX_{L}andXRX_{R}, uniformly over allτ∈{0,1}T\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}andς∈{+,−}\varsigma\in\{+,-\}with probability at least 9/10.

Now fork=1,2,3k=1,2,3, since for any real numbersa,ba,b|a+b|k≤(|a|+|b|)k≤2k−1​|a|k+2k−1​|b|k,|a+b|^{k}\leq(|a|+|b|)^{k}\leq 2^{k-1}|a|^{k}+2^{k-1}|b|^{k},

it follows that⟨|Bgad−(ας−ας¯)​n|k⟩G,ς,τ≤2k−1​⟨|XL−ας​n|k⟩G,ς,τ+2k−1​⟨|ας¯​n−XR|k⟩G,ς,τ=𝒪​((n​log⁡n)k/2).\left\langle|B_{\mathrm{gad}}-(\alpha_{\varsigma}-\alpha_{\bar{\varsigma}})n|^{k}\right\rangle_{G,\varsigma,\tau}\leq 2^{k-1}\left\langle\left|X_{L}-\alpha_{\varsigma}n\right|^{k}\right\rangle_{G,\varsigma,\tau}+2^{k-1}\left\langle|\alpha_{\bar{\varsigma}}n-X_{R}|^{k}\right\rangle_{G,\varsigma,\tau}=\mathcal{O}((n\log n)^{k/2}).

We now prove the exponential moment bounds. Since|T|=o​(t2​n)|T|=o(t^{2}n), we also have|T|=o​((2​t)2​n)|T|=o\!\left((2t)^{2}n\right),
so the previous lemma may be applied with parameter2​t2t.
By Cauchy–Schwarz,⟨et​(Bgad−(ας−ας¯)​n)⟩G,ς,τ≤⟨e2​t​(XL−ας​n)⟩G,ς,τ1/2​⟨e2​t​(ας¯​n−XR)⟩G,ς,τ1/2≤exp⁡(4​C​t2​n),\left\langle e^{t(B_{\mathrm{gad}}-(\alpha_{\varsigma}-\alpha_{\bar{\varsigma}})n)}\right\rangle_{G,\varsigma,\tau}\leq\left\langle e^{2t(X_{L}-\alpha_{\varsigma}n)}\right\rangle_{G,\varsigma,\tau}^{1/2}\left\langle e^{2t(\alpha_{\bar{\varsigma}}n-X_{R})}\right\rangle_{G,\varsigma,\tau}^{1/2}\leq\exp(4Ct^{2}n),

the other bound follows similarly.

LetH^sG\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}denote the graph obtained fromHsGH^{G}_{s}by deleting all inter-gadget edges; thusH^sG\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}is the disjoint union of thehhgadget copies and the isolated vertices. Letℰ\mathcal{E}be the set of inter-gadget edges. The isolated vertices are included to give an explicit independent variance source for the global imbalance. More precisely, under the hard-core measure onH^sG\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}, even after conditioning on the gadget phases and terminal occupations, the isolated vertices remain independent Bernoulli random variables with parameterλ/(1+λ)\lambda/(1+\lambda).

LetLisoL_{\mathrm{iso}}andRisoR_{\mathrm{iso}}denote the isolated vertices on the left and right side ofH^sG\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}.
For a configurationσ\sigmaonH^sG\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}, define the imbalance of those isolated vertices asBiso​(σ):=∑u∈Lisoσu−∑v∈Risoσv.B_{\mathrm{iso}}(\sigma):=\sum_{u\in L_{\mathrm{iso}}}\sigma_{u}-\sum_{v\in R_{\mathrm{iso}}}\sigma_{v}.

For each gadget copyGxG_{x}, recall thatBx​(σ):=∑v∈UxLσv−∑v∈UxRσvB_{x}(\sigma):=\sum_{v\in U_{x}^{L}}\sigma_{v}-\sum_{v\in U_{x}^{R}}\sigma_{v}

is its core imbalance. ## Lemma 4.6.

ForY∈{+,−}V​(H)Y\in\{+,-\}^{V(H)}, setν0​(Y):=n​∑x∈V​(H)(αYx−αY¯x)=n​(α+−α−)​D​(Y).\nu_{0}(Y):=n\sum_{x\in V(H)}(\alpha_{Y_{x}}-\alpha_{\bar{Y}_{x}})=n(\alpha_{+}-\alpha_{-})D(Y).

uniformly overY∈{+,−}V​(H)Y\in\{+,-\}^{V(H)}andτ∈{0,1}T\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}:μH^sG,Y,τ​(|(B−Brem)−ν0​(Y)|≥δ​n)≤exp⁡(−c​n/h).\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}\left(|(B-B_{\mathrm{rem}})-\nu_{0}(Y)|\geq\delta n\right)\leq\exp(-cn/h).

We prove the upper tail; the proof for the lower tail is identical. We first handleBisoB_{\mathrm{iso}}. uniformly for all|t|≤t0​(λ)|t|\leq t_{0}(\lambda).

Hereτx\tau_{x}denotes the restriction ofτ\tautoTxT_{x}, and the use of
the corollary is legitimate because|Tx|=O​(nθ)=o​(n​t2)=o​(n/h2)|T_{x}|=O(n^{\theta})=o(nt^{2})=o(n/h^{2}). Choosetδ=δ/(2​C∗​h)t_{\delta}=\delta/(2C_{*}h). Chernoff’s bound givesμH^sG,Y,τ​(B−Brem≥ν0​(Y)+δ​n)≤e−t​δ​n​⟨et​((B−Brem)−ν0​(Y))⟩H^sG,Y,τ≤e−t​δ​n+C∗​t2​n​h=e−δ2​n4​C∗​h.\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B-B_{\mathrm{rem}}\geq\nu_{0}(Y)+\delta n)\leq e^{-t\delta n}\Bigl\langle e^{t((B-B_{\mathrm{rem}})-\nu_{0}(Y))}\Bigr\rangle_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}\leq e^{-t\delta n+C_{*}t^{2}nh}=e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{4C_{*}h}}.

The next estimates treat the complementary case in which the phase vector is balanced. We
no longer need a full local central limit theorem for the total imbalance. Instead, it is enough to
use the isolated vertices as an explicit smoothing variable. The first lemma records the elementary
point-probability bounds for the isolated contribution, and the second lemma transfers these bounds
to the full decoupled graphH^sG\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}.

## Lemma 4.7.

Fixp∈(0,1)p\in(0,1). There exist constantsδ,c,C>0\delta,c,C>0, depending only onpp, such that the following holds. Letssbe a positive even integer, and letXL,XRX_{L},X_{R}be independentBin​(s/2,p)\text{Bin}(s/2,p)random variables. Then, for every integeraa,ℙ​(XL−XR=a)≤Cs.\mathbb{P}(X_{L}-X_{R}=a)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{s}}.

Moreover, for every integeraawith|a|≤δ​s|a|\leq\delta s,ℙ​(XL−XR=a)≥cs​exp⁡(−C​a2s).\mathbb{P}(X_{L}-X_{R}=a)\geq\frac{c}{\sqrt{s}}\exp\left(-C\frac{a^{2}}{s}\right).

## Proof.

WriteM=s/2M=s/2. The upper bound follows from the standard binomial anti-concentration estimatesupjℙ​(Bin​(M,p)=j)≤C0/M\sup_{j}\mathbb{P}(\text{Bin}(M,p)=j)\leq C_{0}/\sqrt{M}. Indeed,ℙ​(XL−XR=a)=∑jℙ​(XR=j)​ℙ​(XL=j+a)≤supiℙ​(XL=i)≤Cs.\mathbb{P}(X_{L}-X_{R}=a)=\sum_{j}\mathbb{P}(X_{R}=j)\mathbb{P}(X_{L}=j+a)\leq\sup_{i}\mathbb{P}(X_{L}=i)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{s}}.

For the lower bound, we use a standard consequence of Stirling’s formula. There are constantsδ0,c0,C0>0\delta_{0},c_{0},C_{0}>0, depending only onpp, such that, whenever|r−M​p|≤δ0​M|r-Mp|\leq\delta_{0}M,ℙ​(Bin​(M,p)=r)≥c0M​exp⁡(−C0​(r−M​p)2M).\mathbb{P}(\text{Bin}(M,p)=r)\geq\frac{c_{0}}{\sqrt{M}}\exp\left(-C_{0}\frac{(r-Mp)^{2}}{M}\right).

Chooseδ>0\delta>0sufficiently small in terms ofpp. For a fixed integeraawith|a|≤δ​s|a|\leq\delta s, letIaI_{a}be the set of integersjjsatisfying|j−(M​p−a2)|≤c1​M,\left|j-\left(Mp-\frac{a}{2}\right)\right|\leq c_{1}\sqrt{M},

wherec1>0c_{1}>0is a small constant depending only onpp. For allj∈Iaj\in I_{a}, bothjjandj+aj+alie in the range where the preceding Stirling estimate applies, for all sufficiently largeMM. Henceℙ​(XR=j)​ℙ​(XL=j+a)≥c2M​exp⁡(−C2​a2M),j∈Ia.\mathbb{P}(X_{R}=j)\mathbb{P}(X_{L}=j+a)\geq\frac{c_{2}}{M}\exp\left(-C_{2}\frac{a^{2}}{M}\right),\quad j\in I_{a}.

Since|Ia|≥c3​M|I_{a}|\geq c_{3}\sqrt{M}, summing overj∈Iaj\in I_{a}givesℙ​(XL−XR=a)\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X_{L}-X_{R}=a)≥∑j∈Iaℙ​(XR=j)​ℙ​(XL=j+a)\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j\in I_{a}}\mathbb{P}(X_{R}=j)\mathbb{P}(X_{L}=j+a)≥cM​exp⁡(−C​a2M).\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{\sqrt{M}}\exp\left(-C\frac{a^{2}}{M}\right).

SinceM=s/2M=s/2, this is the desired lower bound, after adjusting constants. The finitely many small values ofssare absorbed by decreasingcc.
∎

## Lemma 4.8.

Assume the gadgetGGsatisfies the conclusions of Corollary4.5. Supposes=Θ​(n​h)s=\Theta(nh), withs/2s/2isolated vertices added to each side of the bipartition.
Then there is a constantC<∞C<\inftysuch that, uniformly over all phase vectorsY∈{+,−}V​(H)Y\in\{+,-\}^{V(H)}, all terminal configurationsτ∈{0,1}T\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}, and all integersrr,μH^sG,Y,τ​(B=r)≤Cn​h.\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B=r)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}.

Moreover, uniformly over allY∈{+,−}V​(H)Y\in\{+,-\}^{V(H)}withD​(Y)=0D(Y)=0and allτ∈{0,1}T\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T},μH^sG,Y,τ​(B=0)≥e−C​h​log⁡nn​h.\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B=0)\geq\frac{e^{-Ch\log n}}{\sqrt{nh}}.

## Proof.

WriteB=Sfull+Biso,Sfull:=S+Brem,S:=∑x∈V​(H)Bx.B=S_{\mathrm{full}}+B_{\mathrm{iso}},\quad S_{\mathrm{full}}:=S+B_{\mathrm{rem}},\quad S:=\sum_{x\in V(H)}B_{x}.

The random variableSfullS_{\mathrm{full}}contains the entire imbalance contribution of the gadget copies, including all non-core vertices.
SinceH^sG\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}is a disjoint union and the conditioning onY,τY,\tauconcerns only the gadget copies,SfullS_{\mathrm{full}}andBisoB_{\mathrm{iso}}are independent underμH^sG,Y,τ\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}.

Letp=λ/(1+λ)p=\lambda/(1+\lambda). The isolated contribution has the formBiso=XL−XR,B_{\text{iso}}=X_{L}-X_{R},whereXLX_{L}andXRX_{R}are independentBin​(s/2,p)\text{Bin}(s/2,p)random variables. Therefore, by conditioning onSfullS_{\mathrm{full}}and applying Lemma4.7,μH^sG,Y,τ​(B=r)\displaystyle\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B=r)=𝔼​[μH^sG,Y,τ​(Biso=r−Sfull∣Sfull)]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B_{\text{iso}}=r-S_{\mathrm{full}}\mid S_{\mathrm{full}})\right]=𝔼​[μH^sG,Y,τ​(Biso=r−Sfull)]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B_{\text{iso}}=r-S_{\mathrm{full}})\right]≤Cs≤Cn​h.\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{s}}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}.

This proves the upper bound. It remains to prove the lower bound whenD​(Y)=0D(Y)=0. By Corollary4.5, uniformly in the phase and terminal conditioning of a single gadget,|⟨Bx⟩Gx,Yx,τx−n​(αYx−αY¯x)|≤C1​n​log⁡n,VarGx,Yx,τx​(Bx)≤C1​n​log⁡n.\left|\langle B_{x}\rangle_{G_{x},Y_{x},\tau_{x}}-n(\alpha_{Y_{x}}-\alpha_{\bar{Y}_{x}})\right|\leq C_{1}\sqrt{n\log n},\quad\text{Var}_{G_{x},Y_{x},\tau_{x}}(B_{x})\leq C_{1}n\log n.

Summing over the independent gadget copies gives⟨S⟩H^sG,Y,τ=n​(α+−α−)​D​(Y)+O​(h​n​log⁡n),VarH^sG,Y,τ​(S)≤C2​n​h​log⁡n.\langle S\rangle_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}=n(\alpha_{+}-\alpha_{-})D(Y)+O(h\sqrt{n\log n}),\quad\text{Var}_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(S)\leq C_{2}nh\log n.

SinceD​(Y)=0D(Y)=0, the leading phase contribution cancels.
Thus|⟨S⟩H^sG,Y,τ|≤C3​h​n​log⁡n.\left|\langle S\rangle_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}\right|\leq C_{3}h\sqrt{n\log n}.By Chebyshev’s inequality, after increasing the constantC4C_{4}if necessary,μH^sG,Y,τ​(|S|≤C4​h​n​log⁡n)≥c4\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}\left(|S|\leq C_{4}h\sqrt{n\log n}\right)\geq c_{4}

for some constantc4>0c_{4}>0.

On the event𝒜:={|S|≤C4​h​n​log⁡n}\mathcal{A}:=\{|S|\leq C_{4}h\sqrt{n\log n}\}, the bound|Brem|=o​(h​n)|B_{\mathrm{rem}}|=o(h\sqrt{n})implies that, for all sufficiently largenn,|Sfull|=|S+Brem|≤C5​h​n​log⁡n.|S_{\mathrm{full}}|=|S+B_{\mathrm{rem}}|\leq C_{5}h\sqrt{n\log n}.

For all sufficiently largenn, the inequality|a|≤C5​h​n​log⁡n|a|\leq C_{5}h\sqrt{n\log n}implies|a|≤δ​s|a|\leq\delta s, whereδ\deltais the constant from Lemma4.7; this usess=Θ​(n​h)s=\Theta(nh).
Hence Lemma4.7gives, uniformly for all integersaawith|a|≤C5​h​n​log⁡n|a|\leq C_{5}h\sqrt{n\log n},μH^sG,Y,τ​(Biso=a)≥cs​exp⁡(−C​a2s)≥e−C6​h​log⁡nn​h,\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B_{\text{iso}}=a)\geq\frac{c}{\sqrt{s}}\exp\left(-C\frac{a^{2}}{s}\right)\geq\frac{e^{-C_{6}h\log n}}{\sqrt{nh}},

becausea2/s=O​(h​log⁡n)a^{2}/s=O(h\log n)throughout this window. Therefore,μH^sG,Y,τ​(B=0)\displaystyle\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}(B=0)=𝔼H^sG,Y,τ​[μH^sG,Y,τ​(Biso=−Sfull∣Sfull)]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}\left[\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}\left(B_{\mathrm{iso}}=-S_{\mathrm{full}}\mid S_{\mathrm{full}}\right)\right]≥e−C6​h​log⁡nn​h​μH^sG,Y,τ​(|S|≤C4​h​n​log⁡n)\displaystyle\geq\frac{e^{-C_{6}h\log n}}{\sqrt{nh}}\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}\left(|S|\leq C_{4}h\sqrt{n\log n}\right)≥e−C​h​log⁡nn​h,\displaystyle\geq\frac{e^{-Ch\log n}}{\sqrt{nh}},

after absorbing the fixed constantc4c_{4}into the exponential term.

## Proof of Theorem4.2.

LetHHbe the input graph forMIN-BISECTION, and constructHsGH^{G}_{s}andH^sG\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}as above.
We writeZHsGbalZ^{\text{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}andZH^sGbalZ^{\text{bal}}_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}for the corresponding balanced partition functions andZH^sGZ_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}for the ordinary partition function of the decoupled graph.
ForY∈{+,−}V​(H)Y\in\{+,-\}^{V(H)}, letZHsGbal​(Y)Z^{\text{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y)be the contribution from balanced independent sets whose gadget phase vector isYY, and defineZH^sG​(Y)Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y),ZH^sG​(Y,τ)Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau), andZH^sGbal​(Y,τ)Z^{\text{bal}}_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)analogously.

We begin by decomposing the balanced partition function according to the induced phase vector:ZHsGbal=∑YZHsGbal​(Y)=∑Y:D​(Y)=0ZHsGbal​(Y)+∑Y:D​(Y)≠0ZHsGbal​(Y).Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}=\sum_{Y}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y)=\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y)+\sum_{Y:D(Y)\neq 0}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y).

Thus it suffices to understand separately the contribution of the phase vectors with the correct macroscopic balance and those with the wrong macroscopic balance.

For a fixed phase vectorYY, the only difference betweenHsGH^{G}_{s}andH^sG\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}is the setℰ\mathcal{E}of inter-gadget edges. Hence, decomposing according to the global terminal patternτ∈{0,1}T\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T},ZHsGbal​(Y)=∑τ∈{0,1}TZH^sGbal​(Y,τ)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv),\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y)=\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v}),(4.14)

sinceτ\tauis compatible with the inter-gadget edges exactly when no edgeu​v∈Euv\in Ehas both endpoints occupied.

Next we separate the exact balancedness constraint from the unconstrained gadget weight. For fixedYYandτ\tau,ZH^sGbal​(Y,τ)=ZH^sG​(Y,τ)⋅μH^sG,Y,τ​(B=0).Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)=Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)\cdot\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B=0).

First supposeD​(Y)=0D(Y)=0and by Lemma4.8, we have that:
there is a constantC>0C>0such that for every sufficiently largennthe following holds uniformly inτ\tau:μH^sG,Y,τ​(B=0)≤Cn​h.\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y,\tau}(B=0)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}.

Hence, for the good phase vectorsYYsatisfyingD​(Y)=0D(Y)=0,ZH^sGbal​(Y,τ)≤Cn​h​ZH^sG​(Y,τ).Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}\,Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau).

Substituting this into (4.14) and summing overYYwithD​(Y)=0D(Y)=0give∑Y:D​(Y)=0ZHsGbal​(Y)≤Cn​h​∑Y:D​(Y)=0∑τ∈{0,1}TZH^sG​(Y,τ)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv).\displaystyle\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)\,\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v}).(4.15)

Now we apply the phase-conditioned nearly-independent terminal law to upper boundZH^sG​(Y,τ)Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)for each suchYY. By Lemma4.3(i) (and the fact that(1+𝒪​(n−2​θ))h=1+o​(1)(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-2\theta}))^{h}=1+o(1)), we haveZH^sG​(Y,τ)=ZH^sG​(Y)​μH^sG,Y​(σT=τ)≤ZH^sG​(Y)​(1+o​(1))​QTY​(τ),Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)=Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y)\,\mu_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s},Y}(\sigma_{T}=\tau)\leq Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y)(1+o(1))\,Q_{T}^{Y}(\tau),

whereQTY​(τ)=∏x∈V​(H)QTxYx​(τTx)Q_{T}^{Y}(\tau)=\prod_{x\in V(H)}Q_{T_{x}}^{Y_{x}}(\tau_{T_{x}})is the product of the single-gadget terminal laws in the prescribed phases.
Absorbing the1+o​(1)1+o(1)factor into the constantCC, we obtain∑Y:D​(Y)=0ZHsGbal​(Y)≤Cn​h​∑Y:D​(Y)=0ZH^sG​(Y)​∑τ∈{0,1}TQTY​(τ)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv).\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y)\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Q_{T}^{Y}(\tau)\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v}).

It remains to evaluate the compatibility probability under the product lawQTYQ_{T}^{Y}.
DefineΓ:=1−q+​q−,Θ+:=1−q+2,Θ−:=1−q−2,Θ:=Θ+​Θ−.\Gamma:=1-q_{+}q_{-},\quad\Theta_{+}:=1-q_{+}^{2},\quad\Theta_{-}:=1-q_{-}^{2},\quad\Theta:=\sqrt{\Theta_{+}\Theta_{-}}.

IfYx=YyY_{x}=Y_{y}, each of the2​k2klinking edges corresponding tox​y∈E​(H)xy\in E(H)is legal with probabilityΓ\Gamma. IfYx≠YyY_{x}\neq Y_{y}, one of the two matchings
contributesΘ+k\Theta_{+}^{k}and the other contributesΘ−k\Theta_{-}^{k}. Since no terminal

Also note thatΘ<Γ\Theta<\Gamma, sinceΓ2−Θ2=(q+−q−)2>0.\Gamma^{2}-\Theta^{2}=(q_{+}-q_{-})^{2}>0.

Substituting this identity into (4.15) yields∑Y:D​(Y)=0ZHsGbal​(Y)≤Cn​h​∑Y:D​(Y)=0ZH^sG​(Y)​Γ2​k​|E​(H)|​(ΘΓ)2​k​cut​(Y).\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y)\,\Gamma^{2k|E(H)|}\left(\frac{\Theta}{\Gamma}\right)^{2k\,\mathrm{cut}(Y)}.

IfD​(Y)=0D(Y)=0, thenYYinduces a bisection of H, by definition of the minimum bisection valuebb, we havecut​(Y)≥b\mathrm{cut}(Y)\geq b, which gives(ΘΓ)2​k​cut​(Y)≤(ΘΓ)2​k​b.\left(\frac{\Theta}{\Gamma}\right)^{2k\,\mathrm{cut}(Y)}\leq\left(\frac{\Theta}{\Gamma}\right)^{2kb}.

Together with an obvious upper bound∑Y:D​(Y)=0ZH^sG​(Y)≤ZH^sG,\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y)\leq Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}},we have∑Y:D​(Y)=0ZHsGbal​(Y)≤Cn​h​Γ2​k​|E​(H)|​(ΘΓ)2​k​b​ZH^sG.\sum_{Y:D(Y)=0}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H^{G}_{s}}(Y)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}\,\Gamma^{2k|E(H)|}\left(\frac{\Theta}{\Gamma}\right)^{2kb}Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}.

Now we control the contribution of phase vectorsYYwithD​(Y)≠0D(Y)\neq 0. By definition,ν0​(Y)=(α+−α−)​n​D​(Y).\nu_{0}(Y)=(\alpha_{+}-\alpha_{-})n\,D(Y).

Sinceα+>α−\alpha_{+}>\alpha_{-}andD​(Y)≠0D(Y)\neq 0, we have|ν0​(Y)|≥(α+−α−)​n|\nu_{0}(Y)|\geq(\alpha_{+}-\alpha_{-})n. Moreover,|Brem|=o​(n)|B_{\mathrm{rem}}|=o(n). Hence, on the event{B=0}\{B=0\}and for all
sufficiently largenn,|(B−Brem)−ν0​(Y)|=|−Brem−ν0​(Y)|≥δ​n\left|(B-B_{\mathrm{rem}})-\nu_{0}(Y)\right|=\left|-B_{\mathrm{rem}}-\nu_{0}(Y)\right|\geq\delta n

for a constantδ>0\delta>0. Therefore{B=0}⊆{|(B−Brem)−ν0​(Y)|≥δ​n}.\{B=0\}\subseteq\{|(B-B_{\mathrm{rem}})-\nu_{0}(Y)|\geq\delta n\}.

Applying Lemma4.6yieldsμH^sG,Y,τ​(B=0)≤μH^sG,Y,τ​(|(B−Brem)−ν0​(Y)|≥δ​n)≤e−Ω​(n/h).\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}(B=0)\leq\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}(|(B-B_{\mathrm{rem}})-\nu_{0}(Y)|\geq\delta n)\leq e^{-\Omega(n/h)}.

Consequently,∑Y:D​(Y)≠0ZHsGbal​(Y)\displaystyle\sum_{Y:D(Y)\neq 0}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H_{s}^{G}}(Y)=∑Y:D​(Y)≠0∑τ∈{0,1}TZH^sGbal​(Y,τ)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv)\displaystyle=\sum_{Y:D(Y)\neq 0}\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}(Y,\tau)\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v})=∑Y:D​(Y)≠0∑τ∈{0,1}TZH^sG​(Y,τ)​μH^sG,Y,τ​(B=0)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv)\displaystyle=\sum_{Y:D(Y)\neq 0}\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}(Y,\tau)\,\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}(B=0)\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v})≤e−Ω​(n/h)​∑Y:D​(Y)≠0∑τ∈{0,1}TZH^sG​(Y,τ)\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Omega(n/h)}\sum_{Y:D(Y)\neq 0}\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}(Y,\tau)≤e−Ω​(n/h)​ZH^sG.\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Omega(n/h)}Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}.

Thus the total contribution of the non-balanced phase vectors is exponentially small.

Combining this with the estimate for the balanced phase vectors givesZHsGbal≤(Cn​h​Γ2​k​|E​(H)|​(ΘΓ)2​k​b+e−Ω​(n/h))​ZH^sG.Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H_{s}^{G}}\leq\left(\frac{C}{\sqrt{nh}}\,\Gamma^{2k|E(H)|}\left(\frac{\Theta}{\Gamma}\right)^{2kb}+e^{-\Omega(n/h)}\right)Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}.(4.16)

We now prove the matching lower bound.

By the lower bound of Lemma4.8, for everyτ∈{0,1}T\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}and some constantC>0C>0μH^sG,Y⋆,τ​(B=0)≥e−C​h​log⁡nn​h.\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y^{\star},\tau}(B=0)\geq\frac{e^{-Ch\log n}}{\sqrt{nh}}.

Using the phase-conditioned terminal law as before, we obtain the following lower boundZHsGbal\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H_{s}^{G}}≥ZHsGbal​(Y⋆)\displaystyle\geq Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{H_{s}^{G}}(Y^{\star})=∑τ∈{0,1}TZH^sGbal​(Y⋆,τ)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv)\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Z^{\mathrm{bal}}_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}(Y^{\star},\tau)\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v})=∑τ∈{0,1}TZH^sG​(Y⋆,τ)​μH^sG,Y⋆,τ​(B=0)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv)\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}(Y^{\star},\tau)\,\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y^{\star},\tau}(B=0)\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v})≥e−C​h​log⁡nn​h​∑τ∈{0,1}TZH^sG​(Y⋆,τ)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv)\displaystyle\geq\frac{e^{-Ch\log n}}{\sqrt{nh}}\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}(Y^{\star},\tau)\,\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v})≥e−C​h​log⁡nn​h​ZH^sG​(Y⋆)​∑τ∈{0,1}TQTY⋆​(τ)​∏u​v∈ℰ(1−τu​τv)\displaystyle\geq\frac{e^{-Ch\log n}}{\sqrt{nh}}Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}(Y^{\star})\sum_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T}}Q_{T}^{Y^{\star}}(\tau)\prod_{uv\in\mathcal{E}}(1-\tau_{u}\tau_{v})=e−C​h​log⁡nn​h​ZH^sG​(Y⋆)​Γ2​k​|E​(H)|​(ΘΓ)2​k​b.\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-Ch\log n}}{\sqrt{nh}}\,Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}(Y^{\star})\,\Gamma^{2k|E(H)|}\left(\frac{\Theta}{\Gamma}\right)^{2kb}.(4.17)

We now compareZH^sG​(Y⋆)Z_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}}(Y^{\star})estimates toZH^sGZ_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}.
SinceH^sG\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}is the disjoint union ofhhgadget copies andssisolated vertices,ZH^sG=(1+λ)s​(ZG,++ZG,−)h.Z_{\widehat{H}^{G}_{s}}=(1+\lambda)^{s}(Z_{G,+}+Z_{G,-})^{h}.

The conclusions of Lemma4.3hold with probability at

Thus one execution of the counting reduction succeeds with probability at

Independent repetition and majority vote amplify this probability in the

since an FPRAS gives a much stronger approximation than aneNζe^{N^{\zeta}}-factor approximation.

becausek≫h​log⁡nk\gg h\log n.
Therefore, after independently tossing a fair auxiliary sign for every tied
gadget copy, a sampler within, say, total variation distance1/101/10would,
with probability bounded away from zero, yield a phase vector that is a

## 5Hardness for Fixed Slice Sampling

In this section we prove Theorem1.1. The reduction uses the same phase-coexistence gadget construction and terminal-compatibility calculation as Section4; the new ingredient is the centering
of a two-dimensional fixed slice.

Letλ0:=λ​(α)\lambda_{0}:=\lambda(\alpha), and setd0:=α+​(λ0)−α−​(λ0),δ:=αL−αR.d_{0}:=\alpha_{+}(\lambda_{0})-\alpha_{-}(\lambda_{0}),\qquad\delta:=\alpha_{L}-\alpha_{R}.

Chooseλ⋆∈(λc​(Δ),λ0)\lambda_{\star}\in(\lambda_{c}(\Delta),\lambda_{0})sufficiently close toλ0\lambda_{0}, and writea±:=α±​(λ⋆),d:=a+−a−,p:=λ⋆1+λ⋆.a_{\pm}:=\alpha_{\pm}(\lambda_{\star}),\qquad d:=a_{+}-a_{-},\qquad p:=\frac{\lambda_{\star}}{1+\lambda_{\star}}.

Sinceλ⋆<λ0\lambda_{\star}<\lambda_{0}is close toλ0\lambda_{0}, we have(a++a−)/2<α(a_{+}+a_{-})/2<\alphaandp>αp>\alpha. Defineu:=α−(a++a−)/2p−α>0,γ:=12​(1+δ​(1+u)d).u:=\frac{\alpha-(a_{+}+a_{-})/2}{p-\alpha}>0,\qquad\gamma:=\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{\delta(1+u)}{d}\right).

By takingλ⋆\lambda_{\star}sufficiently close toλ0\lambda_{0}, and perturbing
slightly if necessary, we may assumeγ∈(0,1)∩ℚ\gamma\in(0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}. Finally,
setβL:=γ​a++(1−γ)​a−\beta_{L}:=\gamma a_{+}+(1-\gamma)a_{-}andβR:=γ​a−+(1−γ)​a+.\beta_{R}:=\gamma a_{-}+(1-\gamma)a_{+}.Then we have thatαL​(1+u)=βL+u​p,αR​(1+u)=βR+u​p.\alpha_{L}(1+u)=\beta_{L}+up,\qquad\alpha_{R}(1+u)=\beta_{R}+up.(5.1)

TheNP-hardproblem we reduce from isγ\gamma-MEBCwhich is defined below.

## Fact 5.1(γ\gamma-MEBC[16, p. 644]).

Fix any rationalγ∈(0,1).\gamma\in(0,1).Given a graphHHonhhvertices withγ​h∈ℤ,\gamma h\in\mathbb{Z},it isNP-hardto computebγ​(H):=min⁡{|EH​(S,V​(H)∖S)|:|S|=γ​h}.b_{\gamma}(H):=\min\left\{|E_{H}(S,V(H)\setminus S)|:|S|=\gamma h\right\}.

LetHHbe an input graph onhhvertices withγ​h∈ℤ\gamma h\in\mathbb{Z}. Setj0:=γ​h,D0:=2​j0−h=(2​γ−1)​h.j_{0}:=\gamma h,\qquad D_{0}:=2j_{0}-h=(2\gamma-1)h.

Except for the choice of fugacity and the number of isolated vertices, we use
the same construction as in Section4. In particular, the graphHsGH_{s}^{G}, the decoupled graphH^sG\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}, the global terminal setTT, the inter-gadget edge setℰ\mathcal{E}, the product terminal lawQTYQ_{T}^{Y},
and the compatibility factorsΓ,Θ\Gamma,\Thetaare defined as in

phase vectors withD​(Y)=D0D(Y)=D_{0}, rather than phase vectors withD​(Y)=0D(Y)=0.

We retain the balance notation from Section4. For each gadget copyGxG_{x}, letXL,x:=|I∩UxL|,XR,x:=|I∩UxR|,Bx:=XL,x−XR,x,X_{L,x}:=|I\cap U_{x}^{L}|,\qquad X_{R,x}:=|I\cap U_{x}^{R}|,\qquad B_{x}:=X_{L,x}-X_{R,x},

soXL,x,XR,xX_{L,x},X_{R,x}andBxB_{x}are the core occupation variables and core imbalance used in Section4. LetXL,xrem:=|I∩(L​(Gx)∖UxL)|,XR,xrem:=|I∩(R​(Gx)∖UxR)|.X_{L,x}^{\mathrm{rem}}:=|I\cap(L(G_{x})\setminus U_{x}^{L})|,\qquad X_{R,x}^{\mathrm{rem}}:=|I\cap(R(G_{x})\setminus U_{x}^{R})|.

Accordingly, the non-core imbalanceBremB_{\mathrm{rem}}defined in Section4isBrem=∑x∈V​(H)(XL,xrem−XR,xrem).B_{\mathrm{rem}}=\sum_{x\in V(H)}\left(X_{L,x}^{\mathrm{rem}}-X_{R,x}^{\mathrm{rem}}\right).

LetJLJ_{L}andJRJ_{R}be the occupation counts of the isolated vertices on the left and right sides, respectively, and setBiso:=JL−JR.B_{\mathrm{iso}}:=J_{L}-J_{R}.

The full side-occupation counts onH^sG\widehat{H}_{s}^{G}areXL:=∑x∈V​(H)(XL,x+XL,xrem)+JL,XR:=∑x∈V​(H)(XR,x+XR,xrem)+JR,X_{L}:=\sum_{x\in V(H)}\left(X_{L,x}+X_{L,x}^{\mathrm{rem}}\right)+J_{L},\qquad X_{R}:=\sum_{x\in V(H)}\left(X_{R,x}+X_{R,x}^{\mathrm{rem}}\right)+J_{R},

and we write𝐗:=(XL,XR)\mathbf{X}:=(X_{L},X_{R}). As in Section4,BBdenotes the true full imbalance, soB:=XL−XR=∑x∈V​(H)Bx+Biso+Brem.B:=X_{L}-X_{R}=\sum_{x\in V(H)}B_{x}+B_{\mathrm{iso}}+B_{\mathrm{rem}}.

Thus the event𝐗=(rL,rR)\mathbf{X}=(r_{L},r_{R})is exactly the target fixed-slice event, and on this eventB=rL−rRB=r_{L}-r_{R}.
The moment and exponential-moment estimates from Lemma4.4and Corollary4.5apply to the core variables, while the full constraint is expressed throughBBand𝐗\mathbf{X}.

To adapt the hardness reduction utilized in the previous section, we need two inputs: an exponential penalty when the global phase is not aligned with the fixed slice and a sub-exponential penalty when the global phase is aligned with it. For the first input, we have the following.

where the last identity follows from the definition ofγ\gamma. IfD​(Y)≠D0D(Y)\neq D_{0}, then|D​(Y)−D0|≥2|D(Y)-D_{0}|\geq 2, and hence|n​d​D​(Y)−(rL−rR)|≥d​n−O​(h​n)≥d2​n|ndD(Y)-(r_{L}-r_{R})|\geq dn-O(h\sqrt{n})\geq\frac{d}{2}n

We now show that whenD​(Y)=D0D(Y)=D_{0}, we incur only a sub-exponential penalty.

## Lemma 5.3.

There exists a constantC<∞C<\inftysuch that, for all sufficiently largenn, providedGGsatisfies the simultaneous left–right moment estimates
used in the proof of Corollary4.5, uniformly over
every phase vectorY∈{+,−}V​(H)Y\in\{+,-\}^{V(H)}withD​(Y)=D0D(Y)=D_{0}and
every terminal configurationτ∈{0,1}T\tau\in\{0,1\}^{T},e−C​h​log⁡nn​h≤μH^sG,Y,τ​(𝐗=(rL,rR))≤Cn​h.\frac{e^{-Ch\log n}}{nh}\leq\mu_{\widehat{H}_{s}^{G},Y,\tau}\left(\mathbf{X}=(r_{L},r_{R})\right)\leq\frac{C}{nh}.

## Proof.

Define the core gadget countsSL:=∑x∈V​(H)XL,x,SR:=∑x∈V​(H)XR,x,S_{L}:=\sum_{x\in V(H)}X_{L,x},\qquad S_{R}:=\sum_{x\in V(H)}X_{R,x},

and the non-core gadget countsSLrem:=∑x∈V​(H)XL,xrem,SRrem:=∑x∈V​(H)XR,xrem.S_{L}^{\mathrm{rem}}:=\sum_{x\in V(H)}X_{L,x}^{\mathrm{rem}},\qquad S_{R}^{\mathrm{rem}}:=\sum_{x\in V(H)}X_{R,x}^{\mathrm{rem}}.

WriteS~L:=SL+SLrem,S~R:=SR+SRrem\widetilde{S}_{L}:=S_{L}+S_{L}^{\mathrm{rem}},\qquad\widetilde{S}_{R}:=S_{R}+S_{R}^{\mathrm{rem}}

for the full contributions of all gadget vertices.

approximations. Therefore such an FPRAS would imply a randomized polynomial-time
algorithm forγ\gamma-MEBC: together with the gadget event, its
success probability is at least(89/100)​(9/10)>1/2(89/100)(9/10)>1/2, and standard repetition
amplifies it. Standard search-to-decision followed by deterministic
verification yields an RP algorithm. Thus no such FPRAS exists unless𝐍𝐏=𝐑𝐏\mathbf{NP}=\mathbf{RP}.

On the same event𝒢\mathcal{G}, the same estimates rule out an efficient
sampler. Under the exact fixed-slice measure onHGH^{G},μHGfix​(D​(Y)≠D0)≤exp⁡(−Ω​(n/h)+O​(k​h2+h​log⁡n))=o​(1).\mu^{\mathrm{fix}}_{H^{G}}(D(Y)\neq D_{0})\leq\exp\!\left(-\Omega(n/h)+O(kh^{2}+h\log n)\right)=o(1).

Among phase vectors withD​(Y)=D0D(Y)=D_{0}, increasingcut​(Y)\mathrm{cut}(Y)by one multiplies the terminal-compatibility factor by(Θ/Γ)2​k(\Theta/\Gamma)^{2k}, while
all phase-counting, point-probability, and gadget-ratio losses contribute onlyeO​(h​log⁡n)e^{O(h\log n)}. HenceμHGfix​(D​(Y)=D0,cut​(Y)>b)≤eO​(h​log⁡n)​(Θ/Γ)2​k=o​(1),\mu^{\mathrm{fix}}_{H^{G}}(D(Y)=D_{0},\ \mathrm{cut}(Y)>b)\leq e^{O(h\log n)}(\Theta/\Gamma)^{2k}=o(1),

sincek≫h​log⁡nk\gg h\log n. Given a sampled independent set, the phase vector
is computed from (4.2) on each copyGxG_{x}, tossing an
independent fair auxiliary sign whenever that copy is tied.
Therefore an efficient sampler within total variation distance,
say,1/101/10from the fixed-slice distribution would output a minimumγ\gamma-cardinality cut with probability bounded away from zero. Repetition would solveγ\gamma-MEBCin randomized polynomial time. This completes the proof.
∎
